## 08:10 CDT — Custody anomaly RECONCILED (false alarm, fb-run1223)
Foilbox flagged nonce 624→625 + ~$0.029 gas-only spend as a possible unauthorized tx. Reconciled onchain:
- X&O claim tx 0xbef2b657… = nonce 623 (verified eth_getTransactionByHash).
- Pixel Family claim tx 0x6663531d… = nonce 624 (verified; block 79950547).
- Current eth_getTransactionCount = 625 (0x271) — exactly sequential, no gap.
- Balance delta 10,653,035,860,000 wei ≈ $0.029 = Pixel Family tx value (7,430,000,000,000 wei) + its gas (158,147) — matches to the wei.
Conclusion: the "anomaly" IS the logged Pixel Family claim; stale pipeline custody entries (recorded pre-claim as '624/unchanged') created the false appearance. No unauthorized signing, no NFT gains, sq#4429 = Foil. No user notification needed.

## 10:23 CDT — UPDATE: code.markets inspection completed (replaces the 09:58 “inspection pending” note)
- Live read-only inspection verified that code.markets **Launch** creates a market for a public GitHub repository the user owns/administers; it creates a tradable repository token and Doppler liquidity pool, not a standalone agent launch.
- The **Agents** nav item is currently disabled and labeled “Coming soon.”

# 2026-10-04

## daily-rabbit-hole
2026-10-04 — The auditors asked the exact question the bug later answered — and still missed it. Radix: a June 2023 RDX Works code cleanup silently broke vault ownership checks in the Radix Engine; Zellic's 2024 audit (33 person-weeks, six findings, zero critical — and whose stated assessment goals explicitly included "could an attacker bypass the badge authorization system to perform unauthorized actions on other blueprints?") reviewed the engine and missed it. Exploited Aug 31, 2026: 26 transactions (16:02–16:57 UTC) drained ~$1.26M (458,915 USDC, 72,420 USDT, 61.08 ETH, 6.35 wBTC, 536.16 SOL, 32.91 BNB + 13,000 XRD for fees), laundered via Hyperlane to Ethereum/BNB/Solana → ETH. Radix Foundation confirmed Sept 17, 2026. The save was not a patch but a protocol suicide: validators deliberately withdrew enough stake to break Byzantine consensus, freezing the chain for 10+ days (resumed Sept 11 with patched software). Foundation's stated lesson: security-critical refactors now get separate review, threat modelling, and dedicated regression tests. Secondary blast: pool imbalances from the drained assets let a third party pull millions of XRD from affected pools at skewed rates.
